from sklearn.svm import SVC, SVR

from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
# Model evaluation metrics

# Dimensionality reduction
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA

# Hypothesis 1.
description = "Vehicles with a higher mileage (>100,000 miles) have a 15% lower average price compared to those with lower mileage."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: mileage and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with higher and lower mileage.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with higher and lower mileage.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    high_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] > 100000]
    low_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] <= 100000]

    high_mileage_prices = high_mileage_vehicles['price']
    low_mileage_prices = low_mileage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(high_mileage_prices, low_mileage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and high_mileage_prices.mean() < 0.85 * low_mileage_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with higher and lower mileage. Vehicles with higher mileage have a 15% lower average price, indicating a depreciation effect. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."
END:

# Hypothesis 2.
description = "Vehicles from luxury brands (e.g., Mercedes-Benz, BMW, Audi) have a 20% higher average price compared to non-luxury brands."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: brand and price.
2. Define luxury brands: Mercedes-Benz, BMW, Audi.
3. Calculate the average prices for luxury and non-luxury brands.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of luxury and non-luxury brands.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    luxury_brands = ['Mercedes-Benz', 'BMW', 'Audi']
    luxury_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'].isin(luxury_brands)]
    non_luxury_vehicles = dataset[~dataset['brand'].isin(luxury_brands)]

    luxury_prices = luxury_vehicles['price']
    non_luxury_prices = non_luxury_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(luxury_prices, non_luxury_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and luxury_prices.mean() > 1.20 * non_luxury_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Luxury brand vehicles command a premium, with a 20% higher average price compared to non-luxury brands. This insight can guide marketing strategies and pricing decisions for luxury car dealerships."
END:

# Hypothesis 3.
description = "Vehicles with automatic transmission (A/T) have a 10% higher average price than those with manual transmission."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: transmission and price.
2. Define transmission types: automatic (A/T) and manual.
3. Calculate the average prices for vehicles with automatic and manual transmissions.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with different transmissions.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    automatic_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'A/T']
    manual_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] != 'A/T']

    automatic_prices = automatic_vehicles['price']
    manual_prices = manual_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(automatic_prices, manual_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and automatic_prices.mean() > 1.10 * manual_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with automatic transmission have a 10% higher average price, reflecting consumer preference for ease of use. This can inform inventory management and marketing strategies for car dealerships."

description = "Vehicles from the year 2020 or later have a 15% higher average price compared to those from earlier years."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: model_year and price.
2. Define the model year groups: 2020 or later and earlier years.
3. Calculate the average prices for vehicles from 2020 or later and earlier years.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles from different model years.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    recent_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] >= 2020]
    older_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] < 2020]

    recent_prices = recent_vehicles['price']
    older_prices = older_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(recent_prices, older_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and recent_prices.mean() > 1.15 * older_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles from 2020 or later have a 15% higher average price, likely due to advancements in technology and features. This insight can guide inventory decisions and pricing strategies for car dealerships."

    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    electric_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Electric']
    gasoline_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Gasoline']

    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and electric_prices.mean() > 1.25 * gasoline_prices.mean())

    return result

# Hypothesis 6.
description = "Vehicles with a clean title have a 10% higher average price compared to those with a salvage title."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: clean_title and price.
2. Define title types: clean and salvage.
3. Calculate the average prices for vehicles with clean and salvage titles.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with different titles.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] == 'Yes']
    salvage_title_vehicles = dataset[(dataset['clean_title'] == 'No') | (dataset['clean_title'].isnull())]

    clean_title_prices = clean_title_vehicles['price']
    salvage_title_prices = salvage_title_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(clean_title_prices, salvage_title_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and clean_title_prices.mean() > 1.10 * salvage_title_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with clean titles have a 10% higher average price, indicating that title history significantly affects vehicle value. This insight can guide buyers in making informed decisions and help dealerships in pricing vehicles accurately."

description = "Vehicles that have not been involved in any accidents have a 20% higher average price compared to those with a reported accident history."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: accident and price.
2. Define accident history: none and reported.
3. Calculate the average prices for vehicles with and without accident history.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with different accident histories.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    no_accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] == 'None reported']
    accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] != 'None reported']

    no_accident_prices = no_accident_vehicles['price']
    accident_prices = accident_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(no_accident_prices, accident_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and no_accident_prices.mean() > 1.20 * accident_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with no reported accidents have a 20% higher average price, reflecting the impact of accident history on vehicle value. This insight can inform buyers and dealerships in pricing and valuing used cars."

# Hypothesis 10.
description = "Vehicles with a white interior color have a 5% higher average price compared to those with a black interior color."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: int_col and price.
2. Define interior colors: white and black.
3. Calculate the average prices for vehicles with white and black interior colors.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with different interior colors.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    white_interior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] == 'White']
    black_interior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] == 'Black']

    white_interior_prices = white_interior_vehicles['price']
    black_interior_prices = black_interior_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(white_interior_prices, black_interior_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and white_interior_prices.mean() > 1.05 * black_interior_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with a white interior color have a 5% higher average price compared to those with a black interior color. This insight can inform inventory and marketing decisions for car dealerships."
END:

# Hypothesis 1.
description = "Vehicles with a clean title have a 15% higher average price compared to those without a clean title."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: clean_title and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with and without a clean title.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with and without a clean title.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] == 'Yes']
    no_clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'].isna() | (dataset['clean_title'] != 'Yes')]

    clean_title_prices = clean_title_vehicles['price']
    no_clean_title_prices = no_clean_title_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(clean_title_prices, no_clean_title_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and clean_title_prices.mean() > 1.15 * no_clean_title_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with and without a clean title. Vehicles with a clean title have a 15% higher average price, indicating a premium for clean titles. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."

# Hypothesis 2.
description = "Vehicles with a diesel engine have a 10% higher average price compared to those with a gasoline engine."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: fuel_type and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with diesel and gasoline engines.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with diesel and gasoline engines.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    diesel_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Diesel']
    gasoline_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Gasoline']

    diesel_prices = diesel_vehicles['price']
    gasoline_prices = gasoline_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(diesel_prices, gasoline_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and diesel_prices.mean() > 1.1 * gasoline_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with diesel and gasoline engines. Vehicles with diesel engines have a 10% higher average price, indicating a premium for diesel engines. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."

1. Select relevant features: accident and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with and without an accident history.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with and without an accident history.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] == 'At least 1 accident or damage reported']
    no_accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] == 'None reported']

    accident_prices = accident_vehicles['price']
    no_accident_prices = no_accident_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(accident_prices, no_accident_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and accident_prices.mean() < 0.8 * no_accident_prices.mean())

    return result

# Hypothesis 6.
description = "Vehicles with a model year of 2020 or newer have a 15% higher average price compared to those with a model year of 2015 or older."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: model_year and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with model year 2020 or newer and those with model year 2015 or older.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of the two groups.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    new_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] >= 2020]
    old_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] <= 2015]

    new_prices = new_vehicles['price']
    old_prices = old_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(new_prices, old_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and new_prices.mean() > 1.15 * old_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with model year 2020 or newer and those with model year 2015 or older. Newer vehicles have a 15% higher average price, indicating a premium for recent models. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."

# Hypothesis 7.
description = "Vehicles with an engine power of 300HP or more have a 25% higher average price compared to those with less than 300HP."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: engine and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with engine power of 300HP or more and those with less than 300HP.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of the two groups.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    powerful_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.extract(r'(\d+)').astype(float) >= 300]
    less_powerful_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.extract(r'(\d+)').astype(float) < 300]

    powerful_prices = powerful_vehicles['price']
    less_powerful_prices = less_powerful_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(powerful_prices, less_powerful_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and powerful_prices.mean() > 1.25 * less_powerful_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with engine power of 300HP or more and those with less than 300HP. More powerful vehicles have a 25% higher average price, indicating a premium for high-performance engines. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."

    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    low_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] <= 50000]
    high_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] > 50000]

    low_mileage_prices = low_mileage_vehicles['price']
    high_mileage_prices = high_mileage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(low_mileage_prices, high_mileage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and low_mileage_prices.mean() > 1.20 * high_mileage_prices.mean())

    return result

2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles from the brand 'Mercedes-Benz' and those from the brand 'Ford'.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of the two groups.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    mercedes_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'] == 'Mercedes-Benz']
    ford_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'] == 'Ford']

    mercedes_prices = mercedes_vehicles['price']
    ford_prices = ford_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(mercedes_prices, ford_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and mercedes_prices.mean() > 1.20 * ford_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles from the brand 'Mercedes-Benz' and those from the brand 'Ford'. Mercedes-Benz vehicles have a 20% higher average price, indicating a premium for the luxury brand. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."

description = "Vehicles with a manual transmission have a 5% lower average price compared to those with an automatic transmission."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: transmission and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    manual_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'M/T']
    automatic_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'A/T']

    manual_prices = manual_vehicles['price']
    automatic_prices = automatic_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(manual_prices, automatic_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and manual_prices.mean() < 0.95 * automatic_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions. Vehicles with manual transmissions have a 5% lower average price, indicating a depreciation effect. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."

# Hypothesis 3.
description = "Vehicles with a higher mileage (above the mean) have a 15% lower average price compared to those with lower mileage (below the mean)."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: milage and price.
2. Calculate the mean mileage.
3. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles above and below the mean mileage.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles above and below the mean mileage.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    mean_milage = dataset['milage'].mean()
    high_milage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] > mean_milage]
    low_milage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] <= mean_milage]

    high_milage_prices = high_milage_vehicles['price']
    low_milage_prices = low_milage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(high_milage_prices, low_milage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and high_milage_prices.mean() < 0.85 * low_milage_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with higher mileage (above the mean) have a statistically significant 15% lower average price compared to those with lower mileage. This insight can help buyers prioritize mileage when making purchasing decisions and sellers to adjust their pricing strategies accordingly."
END:

# Hypothesis 4.
description = "Vehicles from the top 10 brands (by frequency) have a 20% higher average price compared to those from other brands."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: brand and price.
2. Identify the top 10 brands by frequency.
3. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles from top 10 brands and other brands.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles from top 10 brands and other brands.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    top_10_brands = dataset['brand'].value_counts().nlargest(10).index
    top_brands_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'].isin(top_10_brands)]
    other_brands_vehicles = dataset[~dataset['brand'].isin(top_10_brands)]

    top_brands_prices = top_brands_vehicles['price']
    other_brands_prices = other_brands_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(top_brands_prices, other_brands_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and top_brands_prices.mean() > 1.20 * other_brands_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles from the top 10 brands have a statistically significant 20% higher average price compared to those from other brands. This insight can guide brand preferences among buyers and pricing strategies for dealerships and manufacturers."
END:

# Hypothesis 5.
description = "Vehicles manufactured in the last 5 years have a 25% higher average price compared to older vehicles."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: model_year and price.
2. Define the threshold for recent vehicles (last 5 years).
3. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between recent and older vehicles.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of recent and older vehicles.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    recent_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] > (dataset['model_year'].max() - 5)]
    older_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] <= (dataset['model_year'].max() - 5)]

    recent_prices = recent_vehicles['price']
    older_prices = older_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(recent_prices, older_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and recent_prices.mean() > 1.25 * older_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles manufactured in the last 5 years have a statistically significant 25% higher average price compared to older vehicles. This insight can help buyers make informed decisions about the age of the vehicle and guide sellers in pricing newer models."

# Hypothesis 8.
description = "Vehicles with a hybrid fuel type have a 15% higher average price compared to those with a gasoline fuel type."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: fuel_type and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with a hybrid fuel type and those with gasoline.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with a hybrid fuel type and those with gasoline.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    hybrid_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Hybrid']
    gasoline_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Gasoline']

    hybrid_prices = hybrid_vehicles['price']
    gasoline_prices = gasoline_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(hybrid_prices, gasoline_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and hybrid_prices.mean() > 1.15 * gasoline_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with a hybrid fuel type have a statistically significant 15% higher average price compared to those with a gasoline fuel type. This insight can guide buyers interested in fuel efficiency and sellers in highlighting the value of hybrid vehicles."

# Hypothesis 9.
description = "Vehicles with a high-performance engine (HP > 300) have a 20% higher average price compared to those with standard engines (HP <= 300)."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: engine and price.
2. Extract the horsepower (HP) from the engine description and create a new feature.
3. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with high-performance engines and standard engines.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with high-performance engines and standard engines.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    def extract_hp(engine):
        hp = engine.split('HP')[0]
        return float(hp.strip())

    dataset['hp'] = dataset['engine'].apply(extract_hp)

    high_performance_vehicles = dataset[dataset['hp'] > 300]
    standard_vehicles = dataset[dataset['hp'] <= 300]

    high_performance_prices = high_performance_vehicles['price']
    standard_prices = standard_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(high_performance_prices, standard_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and high_performance_prices.mean() > 1.20 * standard_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with a high-performance engine (HP > 300) have a statistically significant 20% higher average price compared to those with standard engines. This insight can guide buyers interested in performance and sellers in emphasizing the power of the engine."

"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    black_interior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] == 'Black']
    other_interior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] != 'Black']

    black_interior_prices = black_interior_vehicles['price']

    result = int(p_val < alpha and black_interior_prices.mean() > 1.05 * other_interior_prices.mean())

# Hypothesis 4.
description = "Vehicles with a higher number of cylinders (>6) have a 10% higher average price compared to those with fewer cylinders."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: engine and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with more and fewer cylinders.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with more and fewer cylinders.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    more_cylinders_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.contains('8|10|12')]
    fewer_cylinders_vehicles = dataset[~dataset['engine'].str.contains('8|10|12')]

    more_cylinders_prices = more_cylinders_vehicles['price']
    fewer_cylinders_prices = fewer_cylinders_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(more_cylinders_prices, fewer_cylinders_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and more_cylinders_prices.mean() > 1.1 * fewer_cylinders_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with more and fewer cylinders. Vehicles with more cylinders have a 10% higher average price, indicating a premium for more powerful engines. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers, especially for models with larger engines."

from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler, MinMaxScaler, LabelEncoder
from sklearn.pipeline import Pipeline
# Commonly used machine learning algorithms
from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression, LinearRegression
from sklearn.tree import DecisionTreeClassifier, DecisionTreeRegressor
from sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestClassifier, RandomForestRegressor, GradientBoostingClassifier, GradientBoostingRegressor
from sklearn.svm import SVC, SVR
from sklearn.neighbors import KNeighborsClassifier, KNeighborsRegressor
from sklearn.naive_bayes import GaussianNB
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
# Model evaluation metrics
from sklearn.metrics import accuracy_score, precision_score, recall_score, f1_score, confusion_matrix, roc_auc_score, roc_curve, mean_squared_error, r2_score
# Dimensionality reduction
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA

# Hypothesis 8.
description = "Vehicles with low mileage (<50,000 miles) have a 20% higher average price compared to those with high mileage."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: milage and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: vehicles with low mileage (<50,000 miles) have a 20% higher average price.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with low and high mileage.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    low_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] < 50000]
    high_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] >= 50000]

    low_mileage_prices = low_mileage_vehicles['price']
    high_mileage_prices = high_mileage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(low_mileage_prices, high_mileage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and low_mileage_prices.mean() > 1.20 * high_mileage_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with low mileage and those with high mileage. Vehicles with low mileage have a 20% higher average price, indicating a premium for lower mileage. This insight can inform pricing strategies and marketing efforts for car dealerships and individual sellers."
END:

# Hypothesis 9.
description = "Vehicles with black interior color have a 5% higher average price compared to those with other interior colors."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: int_col and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: vehicles with black interior color have a 5% higher average price.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with black and other interior colors.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    black_interior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] == 'Black']
    other_interior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] != 'Black']

    black_interior_prices = black_interior_vehicles['price']
    other_interior_prices = other_interior_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(black_interior_prices, other_interior_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and black_interior_prices.mean() > 1.05 * other_interior_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with black interior and those with other interior colors. Vehicles with black interior have a 5% higher average price, indicating a premium for this color. This insight can inform pricing strategies and inventory management for car dealerships and individual sellers."
END:

# Hypothesis 10.
description = "Vehicles from the luxury brand Mercedes-Benz have a 25% higher average price compared to those from the non-luxury brand Ford."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: brand and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: vehicles from the luxury brand Mercedes-Benz have a 25% higher average price.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles from Mercedes-Benz and Ford.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    mercedes_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'] == 'Mercedes-Benz']
    ford_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'] == 'Ford']

    mercedes_prices = mercedes_vehicles['price']
    ford_prices = ford_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(mercedes_prices, ford_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and mercedes_prices.mean() > 1.25 * ford_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles from the luxury brand Mercedes-Benz and those from the non-luxury brand Ford. Vehicles from Mercedes-Benz have a 25% higher average price, indicating a premium for luxury brands. This insight can inform pricing strategies and marketing efforts for car dealerships and individual sellers, especially for luxury models."
END:

# Hypothesis 1.
description = "Vehicles with a manual transmission have a 10% lower average price compared to those with an automatic transmission."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'transmission' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    manual_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'M/T']
    automatic_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'A/T']

    manual_prices = manual_vehicles['price']
    automatic_prices = automatic_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(manual_prices, automatic_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and manual_prices.mean() < 0.9 * automatic_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions. Vehicles with manual transmissions have a 10% lower average price, indicating a preference for automatic transmissions in the market. This insight can inform pricing strategies for dealerships and sellers, potentially offering discounts on manual transmission vehicles to boost sales."

2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with black and white exterior colors.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with black and white exterior colors.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    black_vehicles = dataset[dataset['ext_col'] == 'Black']
    white_vehicles = dataset[dataset['ext_col'] == 'White']

    black_prices = black_vehicles['price']
    white_prices = white_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(black_prices, white_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and black_prices.mean() > 1.05 * white_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with black and white exterior colors. Vehicles with black exteriors have a 5% higher average price, suggesting a premium for black vehicles. This insight can inform marketing strategies, emphasizing the luxury and sophistication of black vehicles to attract higher-end buyers."

# Hypothesis 3.
description = "Vehicles from the year 2020 or later have a 15% higher average price compared to those from earlier years."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'model_year' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles from 2020 and later vs. earlier years.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles from 2020 and later to those from earlier years.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    recent_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] >= 2020]
    older_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] < 2020]

    recent_prices = recent_vehicles['price']
    older_prices = older_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(recent_prices, older_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and recent_prices.mean() > 1.15 * older_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles from 2020 and later and those from earlier years. Vehicles from 2020 or later have a 15% higher average price, indicating a premium for newer models. This insight can inform inventory management and pricing strategies, emphasizing the value of newer vehicles to customers."
END:

# Hypothesis 4.
description = "Vehicles with a clean title have a 20% higher average price compared to those with a non-clean title."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'clean_title' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with a clean title and those with a non-clean title.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with a clean title to those with a non-clean title.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] == 'Yes']
    non_clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] != 'Yes']

    clean_title_prices = clean_title_vehicles['price']
    non_clean_title_prices = non_clean_title_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(clean_title_prices, non_clean_title_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and clean_title_prices.mean() > 1.2 * non_clean_title_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with a clean title and those with a non-clean title. Vehicles with a clean title have a 20% higher average price, indicating that title history significantly impacts vehicle value. This insight can inform appraisal and pricing strategies, highlighting the importance of clean titles in marketing and sales."

2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with leather and non-leather interiors.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with leather and non-leather interiors.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    leather_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] == 'Leather']
    non_leather_vehicles = dataset[dataset['int_col'] != 'Leather']

    leather_prices = leather_vehicles['price']
    non_leather_prices = non_leather_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(leather_prices, non_leather_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and leather_prices.mean() > 1.05 * non_leather_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with leather and non-leather interiors. Vehicles with leather interiors have a 5% higher average price, indicating a premium for luxury features. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers, potentially emphasizing the value of leather interiors in marketing."

# Data manipulation and analysis
from scipy.stats import chi2_contingency
import scipy.stats
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
# Machine learning models and preprocessing
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split, cross_val_score, GridSearchCV

# Commonly used machine learning algorithms

from sklearn.svm import SVC, SVR

# Hypothesis 6.
description = "Vehicles with V6 engines have a 10% higher average price compared to those with V4 engines."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'engine' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with V6 and V4 engines.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with V6 and V4 engines.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    v6_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.contains('V6')]
    v4_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.contains('V4')]

    v6_prices = v6_vehicles['price']
    v4_prices = v4_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(v6_prices, v4_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and v6_prices.mean() > 1.1 * v4_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with V6 and V4 engines. Vehicles with V6 engines have a 10% higher average price, indicating a premium for more powerful engines. This insight can inform inventory management and pricing strategies, emphasizing the value of V6 engines to performance-oriented buyers."
END:

# Hypothesis 7.
description = "Vehicles that have never been involved in accidents have a 15% higher average price compared to those that have been in at least one accident."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'accident' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with no reported accidents and those with at least one accident.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with no accidents to those with at least one accident.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    no_accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] == 'None reported']
    accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] != 'None reported']

    no_accident_prices = no_accident_vehicles['price']
    accident_prices = accident_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(no_accident_prices, accident_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and no_accident_prices.mean() > 1.15 * accident_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with no reported accidents and those with at least one accident. Vehicles with no reported accidents have a 15% higher average price, indicating that accident history significantly impacts vehicle value. This insight can inform appraisal and pricing strategies, highlighting the importance of accident-free history in marketing and sales."

# Hypothesis 8.
description = "Vehicles with mileage below 50,000 miles have a 20% higher average price compared to those with mileage above 50,000 miles."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'milage' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with low mileage (below 50,000 miles) and high mileage (above 50,000 miles).
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with low and high mileage.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    low_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] < 50000]
    high_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] >= 50000]

    low_mileage_prices = low_mileage_vehicles['price']
    high_mileage_prices = high_mileage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(low_mileage_prices, high_mileage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and low_mileage_prices.mean() > 1.2 * high_mileage_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with low mileage (below 50,000 miles) and high mileage (above 50,000 miles). This insight can inform inventory management and pricing strategies, emphasizing the value of low mileage vehicles to customers."

    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

# Hypothesis 3.
description = "Vehicles from the top 5 most frequent brands have a 5% higher average price compared to those from other brands."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'brand' and 'price'.
2. Identify the top 5 most frequent brands.
3. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles from the top 5 brands and other brands.
4. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles from the top 5 brands and other brands.
5. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    top_brands = dataset['brand'].value_counts().head(5).index.tolist()
    top_brand_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'].isin(top_brands)]
    other_brand_vehicles = dataset[~dataset['brand'].isin(top_brands)]

    top_brand_prices = top_brand_vehicles['price']
    other_brand_prices = other_brand_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(top_brand_prices, other_brand_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and top_brand_prices.mean() > 1.05 * other_brand_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles from the top 5 most frequent brands have a 5% higher average price compared to those from other brands. This suggests that brand reputation and market presence significantly affect vehicle prices. Dealers can focus on showcasing top brands to attract price-sensitive customers."
END:

    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    automatic_transmission_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] != 'Manual']

import pandas as pd
# Machine learning models and preprocessing
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split, cross_val_score, GridSearchCV
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler, MinMaxScaler, LabelEncoder
from sklearn.pipeline import Pipeline
# Commonly used machine learning algorithms
from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression, LinearRegression
from sklearn.tree import DecisionTreeClassifier, DecisionTreeRegressor
from sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestClassifier, RandomForestRegressor, GradientBoostingClassifier, GradientBoostingRegressor
from sklearn.svm import SVC, SVR
from sklearn.neighbors import KNeighborsClassifier, KNeighborsRegressor
from sklearn.naive_bayes import GaussianNB
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
# Model evaluation metrics
from sklearn.metrics import accuracy_score, precision_score, recall_score, f1_score, confusion_matrix, roc_auc_score, roc_curve, mean_squared_error, r2_score
# Dimensionality reduction
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA

# Hypothesis 6.
description = "Vehicles with a sunroof have a 5% higher average price compared to those without a sunroof."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'features' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with and without a sunroof.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with and without a sunroof.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    sunroof_vehicles = dataset[dataset['features'].str.contains('Sunroof')]
    non_sunroof_vehicles = dataset[~dataset['features'].str.contains('Sunroof')]

    sunroof_prices = sunroof_vehicles['price']
    non_sunroof_prices = non_sunroof_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(sunroof_prices, non_sunroof_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and sunroof_prices.mean() > 1.05 * non_sunroof_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with and without a sunroof. Vehicles with a sunroof have a 5% higher average price, indicating a premium for luxury features. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers, especially when marketing vehicles with sunroofs."

# Hypothesis 7.
description = "Vehicles with a black exterior color have a 3% higher average price compared to those with a white exterior color."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'ext_col' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with black and white exterior colors.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with black and white exterior colors.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    black_ext_vehicles = dataset[dataset['ext_col'] == 'Black']
    white_ext_vehicles = dataset[dataset['ext_col'] == 'White']

    black_ext_prices = black_ext_vehicles['price']
    white_ext_prices = white_ext_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(black_ext_prices, white_ext_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and black_ext_prices.mean() > 1.03 * white_ext_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with a black exterior color have a 3% higher average price compared to those with a white exterior color. This suggests that black vehicles are perceived as more luxurious or desirable in the market. Dealers can focus on showcasing black vehicles to attract premium customers."

# Hypothesis 8.
description = "Vehicles with a model year of 2020 or later have a 10% higher average price compared to those with a model year before 2020."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'model_year' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with model years 2020 or later and those before 2020.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with model years 2020 or later and those before 2020.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    recent_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] >= 2020]
    older_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] < 2020]

    recent_prices = recent_vehicles['price']
    older_prices = older_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(recent_prices, older_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and recent_prices.mean() > 1.10 * older_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with a model year of 2020 or later have a 10% higher average price compared to those with a model year before 2020. This indicates that newer models are valued more highly due to advancements in technology and features. Dealers can emphasize the modern features and benefits of newer models to justify higher prices."

# Hypothesis 9.
description = "Vehicles with a mileage of 50,000 miles or less have a 15% higher average price compared to those with a mileage of more than 50,000 miles."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'milage' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with a mileage of 50,000 miles or less and those with more than 50,000 miles.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with a mileage of 50,000 miles or less and those with more than 50,000 miles.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    low_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] <= 50000]
    high_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] > 50000]

    low_mileage_prices = low_mileage_vehicles['price']
    high_mileage_prices = high_mileage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(low_mileage_prices, high_mileage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and low_mileage_prices.mean() > 1.15 * high_mileage_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with a mileage of 50,000 miles or less have a 15% higher average price compared to those with a mileage of more than 50,000 miles. This indicates that lower mileage is a significant factor in vehicle valuation. Dealers can focus on marketing vehicles with lower mileage to attract price-sensitive customers."

from scipy.stats import chi2_contingency
import scipy.stats
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
# Machine learning models and preprocessing
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split, cross_val_score, GridSearchCV

# Commonly used machine learning algorithms

from sklearn.tree import DecisionTreeClassifier, DecisionTreeRegressor
from sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestClassifier, RandomForestRegressor, GradientBoostingClassifier, GradientBoostingRegressor
from sklearn.svm import SVC, SVR
from sklearn.neighbors import KNeighborsClassifier, KNeighborsRegressor
from sklearn.naive_bayes import GaussianNB
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans

# Hypothesis 1.
description = "Vehicles with a higher horsepower (>200) have a 10% higher average price compared to those with lower horsepower."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: horsepower and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with higher and lower horsepower.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with higher and lower horsepower.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    higher_horsepower_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.contains('HP', na=False)].apply(lambda x: int(x['engine'].split('HP')[0]) if 'HP' in x['engine'] else 0, axis=1) > 200
    lower_horsepower_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.contains('HP', na=False)].apply(lambda x: int(x['engine'].split('HP')[0]) if 'HP' in x['engine'] else 0, axis=1) <= 200

    higher_horsepower_prices = dataset[higher_horsepower_vehicles]['price']
    lower_horsepower_prices = dataset[lower_horsepower_vehicles]['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(higher_horsepower_prices, lower_horsepower_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and higher_horsepower_prices.mean() > 1.1 * lower_horsepower_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with higher and lower horsepower. Vehicles with higher horsepower have a 10% higher average price, indicating a premium for more powerful engines. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."

# Hypothesis 2.
description = "Vehicles with a clean title have a 15% higher average price compared to those with a salvage title."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: clean_title and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with clean title and those with a salvage title.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with clean and salvage titles.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] == 'Yes']
    salvage_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] != 'Yes']

    clean_title_prices = clean_title_vehicles['price']
    salvage_title_prices = salvage_title_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(clean_title_prices, salvage_title_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and clean_title_prices.mean() > 1.15 * salvage_title_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis shows that vehicles with a clean title have a 15% higher average price compared to those with a salvage title, indicating that a clean title is a significant factor in vehicle valuation. This insight can guide dealerships and sellers in emphasizing the importance of clean titles to potential buyers."

# Hypothesis 3.
description = "Vehicles with manual transmission (M/T) have a 5% lower average price compared to those with automatic transmission (A/T)."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: transmission and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with manual and automatic transmissions.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    manual_transmission_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'].str.contains('M/T', na=False)]
    automatic_transmission_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'].str.contains('A/T', na=False)]

    manual_transmission_prices = manual_transmission_vehicles['price']
    automatic_transmission_prices = automatic_transmission_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(manual_transmission_prices, automatic_transmission_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and manual_transmission_prices.mean() < 0.95 * automatic_transmission_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis indicates that vehicles with manual transmission have a 5% lower average price compared to those with automatic transmission, suggesting that automatic transmissions are more preferred by buyers. This insight can help dealerships in pricing and marketing strategies for manual transmission vehicles."

2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with higher and lower model years.

    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    recent_model_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] >= 2020]
    older_model_vehicles = dataset[dataset['model_year'] < 2020]

    recent_model_prices = recent_model_vehicles['price']
    older_model_prices = older_model_vehicles['price']

    result = int(p_val < alpha and recent_model_prices.mean() > 1.2 * older_model_prices.mean())

    return result

# Data manipulation and analysis
from scipy.stats import chi2_contingency
import scipy.stats
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
# Machine learning models and preprocessing
from sklearn.model_selection import train_test_split, cross_val_score, GridSearchCV
from sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler, MinMaxScaler, LabelEncoder
from sklearn.pipeline import Pipeline
# Commonly used machine learning algorithms
from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression, LinearRegression
from sklearn.tree import DecisionTreeClassifier, DecisionTreeRegressor
from sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestClassifier, RandomForestRegressor, GradientBoostingClassifier, GradientBoostingRegressor
from sklearn.svm import SVC, SVR
from sklearn.neighbors import KNeighborsClassifier, KNeighborsRegressor
from sklearn.naive_bayes import GaussianNB
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
# Model evaluation metrics
from sklearn.metrics import accuracy_score, precision_score, recall_score, f1_score, confusion_matrix, roc_auc_score, roc_curve, mean_squared_error, r2_score
# Dimensionality reduction
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA

# Hypothesis 5.
description = "Vehicles with no reported accidents have a 10% higher average price compared to those with at least one reported accident."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: accident and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with no reported accidents and those with at least one reported accident.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with no reported accidents and those with at least one reported accident.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    no_accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] == 'None reported']
    accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] == 'At least 1 accident or damage reported']

    no_accident_prices = no_accident_vehicles['price']
    accident_prices = accident_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(no_accident_prices, accident_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and no_accident_prices.mean() > 1.1 * accident_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "Vehicles with no reported accidents have a 10% higher average price compared to those with at least one reported accident. This finding underscores the importance of a clean accident history in vehicle valuation, and dealerships can use this information to emphasize the value of accident-free vehicles."
END:

# Hypothesis 6.
description = "Vehicles with lower mileage (10,000 miles or less) have a 25% higher average price compared to those with higher mileage (10,000+ miles)."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: milage and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with lower and higher mileage.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with lower and higher mileage.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    low_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] <= 10000]
    high_mileage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] > 10000]

    low_mileage_prices = low_mileage_vehicles['price']
    high_mileage_prices = high_mileage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(low_mileage_prices, high_mileage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and low_mileage_prices.mean() > 1.25 * high_mileage_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis shows that vehicles with lower mileage (10,000 miles or less) have a 25% higher average price compared to those with higher mileage. This highlights the significant impact of mileage on vehicle pricing, and dealerships can use this to emphasize the value of low-mileage vehicles to potential buyers."

# Hypothesis 7.
description = "Vehicles with a higher engine displacement (3.0L or more) have a 15% higher average price compared to those with a lower engine displacement (less than 3.0L)."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: engine and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with higher and lower engine displacement.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with higher and lower engine displacement.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    higher_displacement_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.contains('L', na=False)].apply(lambda x: float(x['engine'].split('L')[0].split('HP')[-1]) if 'L' in x['engine'] else 0, axis=1) >= 3.0
    lower_displacement_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'].str.contains('L', na=False)].apply(lambda x: float(x['engine'].split('L')[0].split('HP')[-1]) if 'L' in x['engine'] else 0, axis=1) < 3.0

    higher_displacement_prices = dataset[higher_displacement_vehicles]['price']
    lower_displacement_prices = dataset[lower_displacement_vehicles]['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(higher_displacement_prices, lower_displacement_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and higher_displacement_prices.mean() > 1.15 * lower_displacement_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant 15% higher average price for vehicles with a higher engine displacement (3.0L or more). This indicates that larger engines are perceived as more valuable, and dealerships can use this insight to price and market vehicles with larger engines more effectively."

description = "Vehicles with a black exterior color have a 5% higher average price compared to those with other exterior colors."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: ext_col and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with black exterior and those with other exterior colors.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with black exterior and those with other exterior colors.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    black_exterior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['ext_col'] == 'Black']
    other_exterior_vehicles = dataset[dataset['ext_col'] != 'Black']

    black_exterior_prices = black_exterior_vehicles['price']
    other_exterior_prices = other_exterior_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(black_exterior_prices, other_exterior_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and black_exterior_prices.mean() > 1.05 * other_exterior_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis shows that vehicles with a black exterior color have a 5% higher average price compared to those with other exterior colors. This suggests that black is a preferred color for buyers, and dealerships can use this information to price and market black vehicles more effectively."

# Hypothesis 10.
description = "Vehicles with a diesel fuel type have a 20% higher average price compared to those with a gasoline fuel type."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: fuel_type and price.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with diesel and gasoline fuel types.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with diesel and gasoline fuel types.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    diesel_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Diesel']
    gasoline_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Gasoline']

    diesel_prices = diesel_vehicles['price']
    gasoline_prices = gasoline_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(diesel_prices, gasoline_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and diesel_prices.mean() > 1.2 * gasoline_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis shows that vehicles with a diesel fuel type have a 20% higher average price compared to those with a gasoline fuel type. This suggests that diesel vehicles are perceived as more valuable, possibly due to their efficiency and performance. Dealerships can use this insight to price and market diesel vehicles more effectively."

# Hypothesis 1.
description = "Vehicles with clean titles have a 10% higher average price compared to those without clean titles."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: `clean_title` and `price`.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with clean titles and those without.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with clean titles and those without.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] == 'Yes']
    no_clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] != 'Yes']

    clean_title_prices = clean_title_vehicles['price']
    no_clean_title_prices = no_clean_title_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(clean_title_prices, no_clean_title_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and clean_title_prices.mean() > 1.1 * no_clean_title_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with clean titles and those without. Vehicles with clean titles have a 10% higher average price, indicating a premium for legal and safety assurance. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers, emphasizing the value of clean titles in marketing and sales."

# Hypothesis 2.
description = "Vehicles with manual transmission have a 5% lower average price compared to those with automatic transmission."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: `transmission` and `price`.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with manual transmission and automatic transmission.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with manual transmission and automatic transmission.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    manual_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'M/T']
    automatic_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'A/T']

    manual_prices = manual_vehicles['price']
    automatic_prices = automatic_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(manual_prices, automatic_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and manual_prices.mean() < 0.95 * automatic_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis shows that vehicles with manual transmission have a 5% lower average price compared to those with automatic transmission, indicating a preference for automatics in the market. Dealerships and sellers can use this insight to price manual transmission vehicles competitively and focus on marketing their unique advantages, such as fuel efficiency and driving experience."

    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    ford_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'] == 'Ford']
    toyota_vehicles = dataset[dataset['brand'] == 'Toyota']

    return result

from sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression, LinearRegression
from sklearn.tree import DecisionTreeClassifier, DecisionTreeRegressor
from sklearn.ensemble import RandomForestClassifier, RandomForestRegressor, GradientBoostingClassifier, GradientBoostingRegressor
from sklearn.svm import SVC, SVR

from sklearn.naive_bayes import GaussianNB
from sklearn.cluster import KMeans
# Model evaluation metrics
from sklearn.metrics import accuracy_score, precision_score, recall_score, f1_score, confusion_matrix, roc_auc_score, roc_curve, mean_squared_error, r2_score
# Dimensionality reduction
from sklearn.decomposition import PCA

# Hypothesis 4.
description = "Vehicles with less than 50,000 miles have a 20% higher average price compared to those with more than 50,000 miles."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: `milage` and `price`.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with less than 50,000 miles and those with more than 50,000 miles.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with less than 50,000 miles and those with more than 50,000 miles.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    low_milage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] < 50000]
    high_milage_vehicles = dataset[dataset['milage'] >= 50000]

    low_milage_prices = low_milage_vehicles['price']
    high_milage_prices = high_milage_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(low_milage_prices, high_milage_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and low_milage_prices.mean() > 1.2 * high_milage_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis shows that vehicles with less than 50,000 miles have a 20% higher average price compared to those with more than 50,000 miles. This indicates a strong preference for low-mileage vehicles, likely due to perceived lower wear and tear. Dealerships and sellers can use this insight to price and market low-mileage vehicles effectively, emphasizing their condition and reliability."
END:

# Hypothesis 5.
description = "Vehicles from the 2020 model year have a 10% higher average price compared to those from the 2015 model year."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: `model_year` and `price`.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles from the 2020 model year and those from the 2015 model year.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles from the 2020 model year and those from the 2015 model year.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    model_year_2020 = dataset[dataset['model_year'] == 2020]
    model_year_2015 = dataset[dataset['model_year'] == 2015]

    model_year_2020_prices = model_year_2020['price']
    model_year_2015_prices = model_year_2015['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(model_year_2020_prices, model_year_2015_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and model_year_2020_prices.mean() > 1.1 * model_year_2015_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals that vehicles from the 2020 model year have a 10% higher average price compared to those from the 2015 model year. This indicates a premium for newer models, likely due to advanced features and technology. Dealerships and sellers can use this insight to market newer models at a premium and highlight their advantages over older models."

# Hypothesis 6.
description = "Vehicles with diesel fuel type have a 15% higher average price compared to those with gasoline fuel type."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: `fuel_type` and `price`.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with diesel fuel type and those with gasoline fuel type.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with diesel fuel type and those with gasoline fuel type.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    diesel_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Diesel']
    gasoline_vehicles = dataset[dataset['fuel_type'] == 'Gasoline']

    diesel_prices = diesel_vehicles['price']
    gasoline_prices = gasoline_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(diesel_prices, gasoline_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and diesel_prices.mean() > 1.15 * gasoline_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis shows that vehicles with diesel fuel type have a 15% higher average price compared to those with gasoline fuel type. This indicates a premium for diesel vehicles, possibly due to their efficiency and torque. Dealerships and sellers can use this insight to market diesel vehicles at a premium and highlight their unique advantages, such as fuel efficiency and towing capacity."
END:

COT = """

2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with no reported accidents and those with reported accidents.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with no reported accidents and those with reported accidents.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    no_accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] == 'None reported']
    accident_vehicles = dataset[dataset['accident'] != 'None reported']

    no_accident_prices = no_accident_vehicles['price']
    accident_prices = accident_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(no_accident_prices, accident_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and no_accident_prices.mean() > 1.15 * accident_prices.mean())

    return result

description = "Vehicles with the most frequent engine type (355.0HP 5.3L 8 Cylinder Engine Gasoline Fuel) have a 20% higher average price compared to those with other engine types."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: `engine` and `price`.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with the most frequent engine type and those with other engine types.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with the most frequent engine type and those with other engine types.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    frequent_engine_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'] == '355.0HP 5.3L 8 Cylinder Engine Gasoline Fuel']
    other_engine_vehicles = dataset[dataset['engine'] != '355.0HP 5.3L 8 Cylinder Engine Gasoline Fuel']

    frequent_engine_prices = frequent_engine_vehicles['price']
    other_engine_prices = other_engine_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(frequent_engine_prices, other_engine_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and frequent_engine_prices.mean() > 1.2 * other_engine_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals that vehicles with the most frequent engine type (355.0HP 5.3L 8 Cylinder Engine Gasoline Fuel) have a 20% higher average price compared to those with other engine types. This indicates a premium for this specific engine type, possibly due to its performance and reliability. Dealerships and sellers can use this insight to market vehicles with this engine type at a premium and highlight their performance advantages."

# Hypothesis 1.
description = "Vehicles with a clean title have a 10% higher average price compared to those without a clean title."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'clean_title' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with a clean title and those without.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with a clean title and those without.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] == 'Yes']
    no_clean_title_vehicles = dataset[dataset['clean_title'] != 'Yes']

    clean_title_prices = clean_title_vehicles['price']
    no_clean_title_prices = no_clean_title_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(clean_title_prices, no_clean_title_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and clean_title_prices.mean() > 1.10 * no_clean_title_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis reveals a statistically significant difference in prices between vehicles with a clean title and those without. Vehicles with a clean title have a 10% higher average price, indicating a premium for vehicles with no legal issues. This insight can inform pricing strategies for car dealerships and individual sellers."
END:

# Hypothesis 3.
description = "Vehicles with an automatic transmission have a 5% higher average price compared to those with a manual transmission."
COT = """
1. Select relevant features: 'transmission' and 'price'.
2. Define the hypothesis: average price difference between vehicles with an automatic transmission and those with a manual transmission.
3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with an automatic transmission and those with a manual transmission.
4. Interpret the results to determine the significance of the observed difference.
"""
def test(dataset):
    import pandas as pd
    from scipy.stats import ttest_ind

    automatic_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'A/T']
    manual_vehicles = dataset[dataset['transmission'] == 'M/T']

    automatic_prices = automatic_vehicles['price']
    manual_prices = manual_vehicles['price']

    t_stat, p_val = ttest_ind(automatic_prices, manual_prices)
    alpha = 0.05
    result = int(p_val < alpha and automatic_prices.mean() > 1.05 * manual_prices.mean())

    return result
insights = "The analysis indicates that vehicles with an automatic transmission have a 5% higher average price compared to those with a manual transmission. This suggests that automatic transmission is preferred by many buyers, and dealers can emphasize this feature to justify higher prices."

3. Perform a two-sample t-test to compare the prices of vehicles with a model year of 2020 or later and those with a model year before 2020.
